拉美市場：巴西通膨改善支撐市場表現，惟總統大選不確定性仍存
7 月全球股市普遍面臨回檔，然而拉美股市表現卻相對抗跌，主因巴西市場先前已因降息預期收
斂而出現修正，而近期巴西通膨數據及通膨預期出現改善，使市場對於巴西通膨問題的擔憂有所緩
解，也帶動巴西指數表現相對硬挺，並為整體拉美市場指數提供支撐。
7 月巴西通膨數據出現改善，主要來自食品與燃料價格回落。7 月巴西 IPCA-15 消費者物價指
數月增 0.06%，明顯低於市場預期的 0.22%，年增率亦由 6 月的 4.80% 降至 4.52%。其中，家庭
食品價格由 6 月月增 0.87% 轉為月減 1.14%；燃料價格整體月減 0.90%， 汽油價格月減 0.68%，

帶動整體通膨放緩，並降低市場對近期巴西物價再度急升的疑慮。
此外，本次巴西通膨數據改善亦反映在核心通膨與服務通膨上，使優於預期的數據更接近結構性
通膨改善。五種核心通膨指標平均月增 0.21%，年增率由 4.45% 降至 4.35%。同時，服務通膨月
增 0.41%，低於市場預期的 0.59%，年增率也由 6.25% 降至 5.95%；然而，勞力密集型服務年增
率仍由 7.06% 升至 7.30%，顯示通膨壓力尚未全面消退，後續仍需持續觀察。
另一方面，儘管巴西通膨數據改善 ， 仍未逆轉市場對巴西央行降息幅度受限的預期。截至 7 月 27
日的巴西央行每週市場調查顯示，2026 年市場通膨預期較前期下降 3 個基點至 5.12%，但 2027
年仍上升 2 個基點至 4.22%，2028 年亦連續第二週上升 2 個基點至 3.80%，2029 年則維持
3.50%；四個年度皆持續高於巴西央行 3.0%的通膨目標 。此外 ，市場對 2026、2027、2028 及 2029
年底巴西基準利率的預期，亦分別維持在 14.00%、12.00%、10.50%及 10.00%，顯示市場對通膨

的疑慮仍限制巴西利率正常化的速度與幅度。
除了通膨之外，巴西總統大選是影響拉美市場的另一項關鍵因素。巴西民調機構 Nexus 於 7 月
全國民調顯示，在總統大選第一輪中，魯拉以 42% 領先波索納洛的 33%，與 7 月中旬前次調查
的 42% 對 34% 大致相同；第二輪則為 47% 對 43%，魯拉仍領先 4 個百分點。兩輪調查均由
魯拉領先，顯示其整體勝率仍較高。此外，包含 Datafolha、Genial／Quaest 等民調皆顯示目前魯
拉贏面較高。由於魯拉並非市場偏好的候選人，其較高的連任機率可能壓抑巴西股市表現。因此，儘
管近期巴西通膨降溫有助支撐整體拉美市場行情，巴西選舉風險仍可能抵銷部分利多；同時，在美伊
衝突仍具不確定性的情況下，巴西降息行情亦料將受到壓抑，後市表現預期將落後於全球市場。

科技類股：CSP 廠商資本支出持續加碼，市場更加關注獲利轉化能力
全球科技產業的主要成長動能仍集中於 AI 基礎建設，四大雲端服務供應商資本支出均維持高檔
或進一步上修，顯示資料中心、AI 伺服器、網路設備及雲端容量的擴建週期尚未降溫。Microsoft、
Amazon 與 Google 的雲端業務均維持強勁成長，且訂單、產能需求與客戶使用量持續增加，反映企
業導入生成式 AI、主權 AI 及 AI 工廠的趨勢仍在深化。市場焦點也逐漸由單一 AI 晶片出貨，延伸至
高速網路、記憶體、CPU、電力與散熱等整體資料中心架構。只要 CSP 廠商未明顯下修資本支出，

AI 硬體供應鏈的訂單能見度便可望維持，NVIDIA、AMD 及相關半導體與設備廠商仍是本輪科技投
資循環的主要受惠者。
不過，市場對科技公司的評價標準正由投入多少資本支出轉向能否將投資轉化為營收、獲利與現
金流。Microsoft 在大幅增加資本支出的同時，營運現金流與自由現金流仍維持成長，Google 雲端
業務的獲利也明顯提升 ， 說明部分AI 投資已逐步進入商業化階段 ；Amazon 則受惠 AWS 成長加速，
雲端業務持續扮演主要獲利來源。相較之下，Meta 與特斯拉因 AI 研發、基礎建設及新事業投入快
速增加，面臨費用上升、利潤率下降與自由現金流承壓的問題。未來科技股表現可能進一步分化，市
場將更重視 AI 收入成長 、 產能利用率 、 投資回收期及折舊費用 ， 而非單純將高額資本支出視為利多。
從供應鏈角度來看，AI 需求正由 GPU 逐步擴散至 CPU、記憶體、晶圓代工、類比晶片與工業半
導體。Intel 資料中心與 AI 業務明顯加速，顯示 CPU 在 AI 伺服器的資料處理、系統控制與通用運算

架構中仍具重要地位。記憶體價格上升雖有利於相關製造商，但也推高 CSP 建置成本，促使雲端業
者重新檢視採購效率與系統配置。整體而言，科技產業有機會由少數 AI 核心企業領漲，逐步轉為伺
服器、網通、儲存、電源、散熱及工業半導體共同受惠的擴散型成長。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但高成長也伴隨高估值與高資本密集度風
險。NVIDIA 的 Blackwell 出貨、Rubin 平台時程、高階記憶體供應與中國市場需求，將是觀察 AI
硬體循環的重要指標；Apple、Meta 與特斯拉則需證明 AI 功能、新裝置及自動化應用能夠轉化為實
際收入。目前 CSP 訂單與雲端需求仍未出現明顯降溫，科技產業基本面維持正向，但隨著折舊、能
源、記憶體及研發成本陸續上升，企業獲利差異可能擴大。未來較具優勢的企業，將不只是擁有先進
技術或積極投入 AI， 而是能建立穩定商業模式 ， 並將AI 投資有效轉化為營收 、 利潤與現金流的公司。

能源類股：美伊衝突帶動油價波動，實體供應仍未見改善
7 月油價波動劇烈，主因美伊衝突與停火談判反覆。美伊在 7 月中旬恢復互相攻擊、美國重啟
對伊朗港口的海上封鎖，且荷莫茲海峽再度發生商船遇襲事件，帶動布蘭特原油一度重返每桶 100
美元以上；然而其後美方暫停攻擊並重啟外交談判後，油價又回落至約 80 美元，這顯示當前油價
變動主要反映市場對於衝突升降溫的預期，而非實際供應已恢復正常。
彭博分析指出，目前石油實物市場尚未出現明顯改善，荷莫茲海峽可觀察到的油輪數量仍遠低於
戰前，許多船隻通行時仍持續關閉船舶自動識別系統，且保險公司、租船人及船東也維持審慎態度，

顯示商業航運、海運保險、運費及出口流量均未恢復正常。航運資料公司 Windward 7 月 30 日的
船舶追蹤也顯示，荷莫茲海峽當日僅有 7 艘船舶進入波斯灣、4 艘離開，整體運量相較於戰前仍有
極大的落差，故一旦美伊談判受阻，地緣政治風險溢價仍有迅速回升的可能性。
另一方面，胡塞武裝宣布將封鎖沙烏地阿拉伯有關的船舶運輸，並對紅海曼德海峽通行發出威
脅，也加劇油價上行風險。彭博指出，沙烏地阿拉伯雖可利用東西輸油管，將原先在荷莫茲海峽受阻
的每日約 500 萬桶原油運往紅海沿岸的延布港，但經由紅海運往亞洲的船舶仍須通過曼德海峽，因
此原油出口風險只是由波斯灣轉移至紅海。船舶追蹤公司 Windward 截至 8 月 3 日的統計顯示，
7 月 20 日後曼德海峽船隻流量下降 22%， 油輪更減少 40%，顯示經紅海的替代路線仍面臨威脅。
然而 ， 高油價帶來的需求破壞也會限制油價上漲空間 。OPEC 7 月月報將 2026 年全球石油需求
量下修至每日 1.0594 億桶，需求年增量由每日 97 萬桶降至 78 萬桶；2027 年需求年增量雖上

修至每日 194 萬桶，但上修主因 2026 年基期較低的緣故，並非終端需求出現改善。
在此背景下，彭博研究將油價分為三種情境：若外交協議落地、荷莫茲航運與保險恢復正常，布
蘭特原油可能回到每桶 65 至 80 美元；若戰事僅暫停、實物供應持續受阻，則較符合每桶 90 至
110 美元的衝突僵局情境；若荷莫茲與曼德海峽同時受阻，油價可能升至每桶 140 美元以上。
展望 8 月，川普宣布將和伊朗展開新一輪談判，儘管隨即遭到伊朗否認，且荷莫茲海峽仍處於受阻
狀態 ， 然而我們認為隨著期中選舉時程逼近 ， 川普將可能暫緩對伊朗的行動 ， 藉此壓低油價挽救選情 ，
在此情境下能源類股將會轉趨區間震盪整理，目前中性看待後市表現。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：央行配置需求仍強，惟 ETF 資金流出壓抑短線表現
7 月金價保持在區間內震盪整理，儘管美元走強及美債殖利率攀升持續為金價帶來壓力，然而來
自全球央行的買盤也如我們先前所料的為金價帶來了有力的支撐。
世界黃金協會報告指出，2026 年第二季，全球黃金總需求為 1,269 噸，與 2025 年同期持平；
累計 2026 年上半年，全球黃金總需求年增 2%至 2,522 噸，需求金額創下 3,800 億美元新高。與此
同時 ，2026 年第二季倫敦金銀市場協會黃金午後定盤價平均為每盎司 4,506.29 美元 ， 雖較第一季下

跌 8%，但仍較 2025 年同期上漲 37%。這顯示第二季金價雖自高檔回落，但全球黃金需求並未同步
轉弱，尤其央行買盤回升更成為支撐市場的主要力量。
第二季全球央行合計購買 289 噸黃金，歷經第一季的放緩後，購金規模再次重新回到過去四年
的高檔水準。高盛分析則預估，全球央行 5 月購金量達到 81 噸，其中中國購買 48 噸，是當月最大
的可識別買家；經季節調整後，全球央行近三個月月均購金量達到 67 噸，約為 2022 年前每月 17
噸平均值的四倍 。 上述數據顯示 ， 即使在金價走勢低迷的狀況下，全球央行對於黃金的承接力道仍強 。
進一步觀察央行持續購金的原因，官方貨幣與金融機構論壇（OMFIF）2026 年針對 74 家央行
及 16 家公共退休與主權基金進行的調查顯示，儲備多元化仍是央行持有黃金最主要的原因。此外，
有 51%的受訪者將防範地緣政治風險列為考量，比例較 2024 年增加 11 個百分點；同時，79%的受
訪央行認為全球貨幣體系正朝向多極化發展 。調查結果顯示， 各國央行正逐步降低對單一儲備貨幣的

依賴，並增持黃金以因應制裁、資產凍結及貨幣體系分裂風險，由此也可見，本波全球央行買盤更接
近長期儲備調整，而非短線對於金價的追逐。
然而對於利率較為敏感的私人投資資金 ， 降低黃金配置趨勢仍持續為金價帶來明顯壓力。 世界黃
金協會數據顯示，2026 年第二季全球黃金 ETF 淨流出 45 噸，6 月單月再流出 89 億美元；其中，北
美黃金 ETF 上半年累計流出 77 億美元。造成北美資金流出的主因，在於聯準會態度偏向鷹派，加上
美伊衝突推升通膨疑慮，帶動實質利率與美元走高，進而提高持有黃金的機會成本。
展望後市，儘管短線上金價表現持續承壓，然而中長線看，當前市場已定價到明年底前 Fed 將
升息 2 碼的鷹派走勢，若後續美伊衝突有所緩和，使市場升息預期降溫，黃金 ETF 資金賣壓有望出
現改善， 屆時搭配央行長期買盤 ，黃金仍有望重啟新一波漲勢，故我們對黃金類股後市維持中性看法 。

### Page 11

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：Fed 鷹派暫停推升長率，投資仍以 3~10 年期美債/投級債為主
FOMC 7/29 以 9 比 3 票將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.50%～3.75%。Hammack、Kashkari
及 Logan 三名委員反對維持利率不變，主張本次即升息 1 碼。會後聲明指出美國經濟活動仍以穩健
速度擴張，資本投資及生產力成長強勁，但通膨仍高於 2%目標，部分反映能源等供給衝擊。
Fed 主席 Warsh 強調，本次按兵不動並非聯準會行動遲緩或政策慣性，並重申不存在高於 2%
的 「軟性通膨目標」 。超過五年的高通膨不可能因單月物價小幅下降便獲得解決，聯準會將持續履行

物價穩定責任，必要時不會猶豫採取行動。華許同時指出，距離上次會議的 42 天內，美債殖利率曲
線各期限的名目與實質殖利率均明顯上升，部分升幅已位居近 20 年的前十分位數。他認為，聯準會
減少前瞻指引後，市場開始依據經濟數據自行調整價格。此番談話並非表示聯準會受制於市場，而是
暗示金融市場利率上升已產生部分緊縮效果，使本次不必立即升息。華許指出，AI 相關高科技設備
及軟體投資近四季增長率接近 20%，有助於製造業產出及未來生產力，但記憶體、邏輯晶片及相關
AI 基礎建設價格亦同步上升。FOMC 已開始討論，相關漲價究竟只是特定產業的供給衝擊，或可能
演變為更廣泛的通膨壓力。
債市對此次 FOMC 決議的反應呈現長端領跌、殖利率曲線明顯陡峭化。此一走勢顯示，市場雖
因 Fed 未立即升息而降低短端利率壓力，卻同步提高對能源通膨、財政供給、期限溢酬及政策可信
度的風險定價。利率期貨對 9 月升息的定價在決議後一度升至約 75%，稍後回落至約 6 成，代表市
場並未排除升息，而是等待後續通膨與就業數據確認。8 月沒有例行 FOMC 利率會議，債市定價將

更加依賴經濟數據。由於 Warsh 減少前瞻指引，單一數據對升息機率及短端殖利率的影響可能較過
去放大，預期 8 月殖利率仍將維持高檔震盪。
8 月債市的核心矛盾在於「殖利率已具吸引力，但長端風險尚未完全穩定」。我們認為偏高的殖
利率可提供較佳息收及價格緩衝；若油價維持高檔、核心通膨降溫停滯或美國公債供給增加， 20～
30 年期債券仍可能承受期限溢酬上升壓力。貝萊德亦指出，目前美債實質殖利率已具吸引力，但收
益機會仍應透過存續期間控制與券種選擇取得，而非全面延長年期。
在未來聯準會減少前瞻指引 、 利率路徑更依賴數據環境下 ， 市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將
提高，預期債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較
大波動，且存續期間過短亦面臨再投資風險；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供
給增加等風險。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸
引力的息收，亦能降低短端政策波動及長端期限溢酬風險。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.29 
一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

## Document 077: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業175 社の2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」
（注）26年冬の予測値は第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所

Greater China Technology Semiconductors  | Asia

Cloud Semis: Growing Cloud
Capex into 2027
Hyperscalers’ spending remains strong so far, with further
expansion signaled for 2027 amid FCF turning negative. Positive
read for cloud semis (Aspeed and Montage).
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
2027 capex growth is highlighted: The Big Four hyperscalers' combined C3Q capex
was US$185bn, +90% Y/Y. CY2026 guidance stands at approximately US$170bn for
Microsoft, US$195–205bn for Google, US$135–145bn for Meta, and US$220bn for
Amazon. Google and Amazon maintained guidance; Meta raised its spending floor

US$5bn. More importantly, Microsoft expects spending to accelerate in January–
June 2027, Google anticipates a significant increase in 2027, and Amazon expects
meaningfully higher spending. Meta also plans to keep investing aggressively.
Supply constraints remain the issue: Microsoft reported C3Q26 capex of US
$45.2bn and expects C4Q spending >US$50bn, with some capacity deliveries
shifting into 1H27. Google invested US$51.4bn and turned to third-party computing

late in C3Q to address supply constraints, with usage rising in C4Q. Meta invested
US$34.9bn, including finance lease principal payments; Amazon’s cash capex
reached US$75.9bn. Inference already contributes well over half of AWS AI revenue.
Debating monetization: Microsoft generated US$18.6bn of quarterly FCF and
expects positive FCF in FY2027. Google, Meta, and Amazon reported negative
quarterly FCF of US$9.1bn, US$2.1bn, and US$33.5bn, respectively. Google also

flagged depreciation, data center operating costs, and third-party computing
expenses as near-term cloud margin headwinds, alongside TPU inventory cash
requirements. Management remains focused on attractive long-term returns.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through. Sustained server investment supports
Aspeed's and Montage's demand outlook; delayed capacity could shift revenue
between quarters, but it's just a timing issue. Approximately 75% of Microsoft’s C3Q

capex targeted short-lived assets, including CPUs, GPUs, and higher memory/storage
costs, up from two-thirds mentioned before. Google allocated ~65% to servers and
networking.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
2027 capex is highlighted: Microsoft, Google and Amazon flag higher spending;
Meta remains committed to aggressive investment.
Capacity constraints, not budget: Microsoft’s delivery delays and Google’s third-
party computing usage point to infrastructure bottlenecks.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through so far. Sustained hyperscaler spending

provides a favorable backdrop, with capacity additions raising demand.
November 2, 2026 01:07 AM GMT

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

晶豪科 (3006 TT)  Trading Buy

報價收斂，後續成長動能受限，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸
納三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能 ， 引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口 ，
缺貨潮由 DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND 
Flash 。同業大廠如華邦電 (2344 TT) 基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量，
產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品，對 DDR2 與 DDR3 採取停
供或減供措施，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與
DDR3 合計佔 DRAM 營收比重超過 50% ，在同業減供退出的趨
勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，市占地位結構性擴
張。預估晶豪科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.72 元。雖然記憶體價格
在 2027 年仍可以維持高檔，但目前報價漲幅開始收斂，產能供
給受限，成本持續上揚，後續成長動能受限，因此對晶豪科投
資建議為 Trading Buy 。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.09 元： 受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM、DDR5
等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM、DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供
給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元，
QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ，3Q26 稅後
淨利 107.59 億元，QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。1Q26~3Q26
營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025 年
同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 
64.54 元。  
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元： 展望 4Q26 營運表現，儘管整體
出貨數量受限於上游晶圓代工與封測端產能配額難見大幅擴
增，但由於 4Q26 產品平均銷售單價 (ASP) 延續上漲趨勢，漲幅
約 10~20% 。但晶圓代工從 07/2026 漲約 45% ，封測約漲 20% ，
將會逐步反映在 4Q26 銷貨成本，經營團隊預期 4Q26 毛利率將
穩健維持在 3Q26 之歷史高檔區間，來自產品售價調升幅度足
以抵銷成本增幅。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元。預估 2026 年
稅後 EPS 102.25 元。

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  晶豪科 ESG ：  
企業永續與治理架構  
• 企業永續委員會：於 10/2020 成立，負責評估永續經營的風險與機會、制
訂永續發展願景與策略。  
• 核心推動構面：涵蓋強化公司治理、夥伴關係、環境永續、友善職場及社
會公益。  
• 永續報告書：定期依循國際標準 (如  GRI 、 TCFD 、 SASB 等 )編製與揭露
年度永續報告書 ， 可至晶豪科技 ESG 報告書或耀智永續報告書資料庫  查閱
與下載。  
環境與氣候行動  
• 減碳目標：積極因應全球氣候變遷，設定具體減碳目標，其減碳表現經評
估朝向升溫  1.5°C 以下的目標邁進 (實測減碳溫度約  1.506°C) 。  
• 永續金融連結：曾與金融機構 (如台新銀行、北富銀等 )合作推動永續績效
聯貸案 (30 億元聯貸案 )，將  ESG 指標與授信條件結合，落實綠色金融與企
業社會責任。  
社會責任與人才培育  
• 社會公益與教育：設立「晶豪科技教育基金會」，長期投入教育與社會公
益相關推廣。  
• 人才與實習計畫：積極與青年世代接軌，辦理暑期研發與  ESG 實習生計
畫，深化校園人才交流與職場體驗。  
記憶體價格調漲，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，稅後淨利
107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元：  
晶豪科為國內利基型記憶體  IC 設計公司，產品涵蓋利基型 DRAM 產品組
合，涵蓋從傳統 SDRAM 到主流的 DDR 、 DDR2 、 DDR3 及 DDR4 。廣泛應
用於消費性電子、工業控制、網通設備、 PC 週邊，公司主要是 4Gb 以下為
主。在 Flash 方面，晶豪科有 SLC NAND Flash 以及編碼型快閃記憶體 (NOR 
Flash) 。因應中低階智慧手機、系統級封裝 (SiP) 及固態硬碟 (SSD) 需求快速
成長，公司以 MCP/eMCP/eMMC 為主力產品。類比和數位的產品
(Audio/Power/Motor Driver/Sensor) 佔公司營收比重低。各產品佔比， DRAM
佔營收比重最高，約 60~65% ， Flash 和其他約佔 30~33%(MCP 等也有含
DRAM) ，非記憶體佔營收比重 5%。  
受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM 、 DDR5 等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM 、
DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收
221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ， 3Q26 稅
後淨利 107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。  
累計 1Q26~3Q26 營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025
年同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 64.54 元。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

3 群益投顧 
3Q26 底晶豪科的存貨為 90.76 億元，庫存水準較 2Q26 的 79.56 億元，
QoQ+14.08% ，要反映晶圓廠與封測調價造成的成本墊高，而非量的顯著增
加。  
合作晶圓廠現階段增產幅度相對受限，預估 4Q26 營收 243.02 億元，
QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 39.41 元：  
晶圓代工端產能配置方面，晶豪科 DRAM 主要晶圓代工廠為力積電 (6770 
TT) ，也是力積電記憶體最大的代工客戶。主要代工夥伴力積電因先前將部
分廠房出售予 Micron(MU US) ，現有 12 吋晶圓廠中 P1 與 P2 廠原生產邏輯
代工，在 3Q26 調整至生產記憶體代工，但整體而言，記憶體代工產能釋出
受限，爭取晶圓代工與後段封測產能 (Wafer & Backend) 成為晶豪科當前最
關鍵的營運課題。  
力積電於 07/2026 全面啟動新一波代工報價調漲，其中針對 DRAM 投片價
格大幅調升約 45% 。考量自晶圓投入、前段製造至出貨之生產週期 (Cycle 
time) 約需 5~6 個月，晶豪科配合客戶訂單展開拉貨後，該批高價晶圓成本
將於 4Q26 正式反映於銷貨成本 (COGS) 之中。主要封測代工夥伴南茂 (8150 
TT) 亦自 07/2026 起調漲 20% 之封測費用，受限於約 6 個月之封測與成品週
轉週期，亦遞延至 11/2026 正式認列於營業成本。  
然而，由於市場端面臨華邦電等同業對成熟製程 DDR2 與 DDR3 減供或停
供的結構性缺口，終端訂單滿足率目前僅達 60% 至 70%，晶豪科具備極為強
勁的賣方定價話語權。在 4Q26 產品 ASP 持續走高，且漲幅 10~20% 的帶動
下 ， 晶豪科有能力將晶圓與封測端增加的成本全數轉嫁予客戶端 ， 支撐 4Q26
毛利率穩健維持在 3Q26 之 77.44% 歷史高檔區間。預估晶豪科 4Q26 營收
243.02 億元， QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 
39.41 元。  
預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72 億
元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元：  
晶豪科的產品組合，記憶體佔營收比重最高，約 95%。記憶體當中， DRAM
佔 65% 左右，以 KGD 為主。其他則為 NAND 、 NOR 以及其他
MCP/eMCP/eMMC 等。 DRAM 當中， DDR2 和 DDR3 佔目前 DRAM 出貨量
50% 。  
市場競爭格局方面， AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸納
三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能，引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口，缺貨潮由
DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND Flash 。同業大廠如華
邦電基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量 ， 產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品 ， 對DDR2
與 DDR3 採取停供或減供措施，其中華邦電之 DDR2 僅保留供應特定策略
夥伴，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與 DDR3 合計營收比重
超過 50%，在同業減供退出的趨勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，
市占地位結構性擴張。  
終端需求方面， AI PC、筆記型電腦與智慧型手機單機搭載記憶體容量提升，
加上實體 AI( 如人形機器人與自駕載具 )逐步落地，帶動非 HBM 記憶體需求
持穩增長。消費性電子端雖因記憶體 ASP 漲幅較大出現部分觀望情緒，惟
晶豪科高階與商務應用拉貨依然暢旺，客戶訂單滿足率受限於產能瓶頸，整

### Page 4

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

4 群益投顧 
體營運動能無虞。展望 2027 年營運，管理層指出全球傳統記憶體產能供給
增長極為有限，供需失衡格局短期難以扭轉，預期市場供不應求狀態將延續
至 2027 年全年，支撐產品報價結構性維持堅挺， 2027 年整體營運維持樂觀
基調。預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72
億元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元。  
股利政策方面，管理層指出將經董事會討論定案，過往配發率曾達 50%，預
估未來現金股利發放率將落在 30% 至 50% 區間。  
Forward PE Band

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

## Document 307: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

### Page 4

4 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 315: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-07)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 345: First Impressions_ RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 16

### Page 1

#rbnz #newzealand
First Impressions: RBNZ Monetary Policy Review October 2026
By Kelly Eckhold & Westpac New Zealand Economics
12:30 October 28 2026 Share
The RBNZ left the OCR unchanged at 2.75% (in contrast to market expectation for a hike,
including ourselves).
The MPC has stuck to its strategy of pausing to assess the impact of tightening already done. There is
a clear split among the MPC members on where the risks lie although it seems clear enough that

higher interest rates are in the future the data evolves as the Committee expects. Key factors to focus
on are data on inflation expectations, the global economy (and potential downside risks there) and the

### Page 2

path of fuel prices.  We expect a 25 bp increase in the OCR in December to 3% and are reverting to
the OCR profile we had up until mid last week.  We don't think the December hike is a done deal as the
data will inform the next move. We will revert with more detail after the press conference. 
Key takeouts from the minutes below.

Media contact
Kelly Eckhold
Chief economist 
+64 9 348 9382
+64 21 786 758
Key takeouts
Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The next policy
decision
Future policy decisions will depend
on the Committee’s judgement of the
balance of risks to medium-term
inflation.
Members discussed that information
arriving over the next six weeks
could meaningfully change the
appropriate policy stance in either
direction.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
All members agreed that tighter
monetary policy is likely to be
required, but remains conditional on
the economic outlook.
  However, the future OCR path is not
pre-determined.

The medium term
direction for the
OCR
The Committee judges that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
appropriate to return inflation to the
2 percent target mid-point while
supporting growth and employment.
Further OCR increases likely, but
are conditional on the outlook

### Page 3

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  All members agreed that gradually
removing monetary stimulus is
consistent with achieving the
medium-term inflation target,
conditional on the outlook.
If domestic pricing pressures fail to
moderate sufficiently or broaden
beyond what costs and normal pass-
through can explain, this could
warrant further tightening.
  Conditional on the central economic
outlook, members judged that the
OCR may need to increase further.
On the other hand, subdued pricing
pressures could require holding at
future meetings.
    And if the global economy turns
sharply down, materially weakening
demand and the inflation outlook,
this could alter the desired stance,
including leading to monetary
easing.

The neutral OCR   The Committee acknowledged that
the neutral interest rate has a wide
range of plausible estimates.
    Members noted that the increase in
global long-term yields may partly
signal a higher global neutral
interest rate, due to higher inflation
expectations, war destruction and
demand for capital to support AI
investment.
    Higher global interest rates could
bring forward a potential global
downturn, and the neutral interest
rate could decline rapidly in the
event of an abrupt global
adjustment.

### Page 4

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The strength of
economic activity
After lacklustre growth in the June
quarter, New Zealand's economic
recovery has most likely resumed
but remains uneven.
The New Zealand economy
continues to recover, although
conditions remain uneven.
  The recovery is expected to
strengthen and broaden.
The Committee noted that GDP
growth was more than expected
through the first half of 2026.
  The Committee assessed that
growth was lacklustre in the second
quarter of 2026.
Business survey measures point to
continued expansion in economic
activity.
  The economic recovery is expected
to have resumed in the third quarter,
while remaining uneven across
sectors and regions.
Household consumption has been
weak and the aggregate saving rate
is estimated to be 3.7 percent,
above the 10-year average of 1
percent.

The global
economic
outlook
The global economy is facing
significant risks that could affect
commodity prices and demand for
exports.
Global growth has been resilient but
is increasingly fragile given
geopolitical tensions, high energy
prices, financial stresses, and
surging AI-related investment.
  Despite recent geopolitical events,
economic growth in New Zealand’s
trading partners has remained
resilient and the outlook has
improved.
The Committee assessed that there
are significant risks to the global
economic outlook.

### Page 5

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  Although risks to the global economy
remain heightened, the Committee
expects global economic conditions
to remain favourable for New
Zealand’s export facing businesses
over the medium term.
The Committee noted that a global
downturn could occur abruptly.

The labour
market
Conditions in the labour market
should improve as the recovery
gathers pace.
Employment growth remains
subdued and higher prices will
further reduce real incomes and
purchasing power.
  Employment growth has not been
sufficient to fully absorb new
entrants into the labour market and
unemployment is elevated,
particularly in Auckland and
Wellington and for youth and the
long-term unemployed.
Employment intentions remain
weak, pointing to continued slow
recovery in the labour market.
  The labour market is expected to
slowly improve, with employment
growing and unemployment falling
as the economy recovers.
To date, wage growth has been
subdued, and medium-term inflation
expectations have remained stable.
  On balance, the Committee
assesses that spare capacity
remains in the economy, particularly
in the labour market.

### Page 6

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
The short term
inflation outlook
Inflation increased to 4.1 percent in
the June quarter because of higher
fuel prices arising from the conflict in
the Middle East.
Annual inflation is expected to
increase to 4.4 percent in the
December quarter, largely reflecting
current futures prices for fuel.
  The Committee expects inflation to
remain elevated this year before
returning to the target band by mid-
2027 and reaching the 2 percent
mid-point later next year.
Near-term inflation forecasts are
uncertain given volatility in prices for
oil and refined fuels.
    Direct effects of higher fuel prices
will drop out of the annual inflation
calculation in late 2027 if prices for
refined petroleum products stabilise
or fall.

The medium term
inflation outlook
Core inflation, expected wage
growth, and inflation expectations
remain consistent with inflation
returning to the 1 to 3 percent target
band by mid-2027 and the 2 percent
target midpoint later next year.
The Committee is focused on
returning inflation to the 2 percent
target mid-point over the medium
term, discounting temporary
disturbances to inflation and
avoiding unnecessary economic
instability.
  Longer-term inflation expectations
remain near 2 percent, while most
measures of one and two-year-
ahead inflation expectations have
fallen since May.
Impacts on shortterm pricing
behaviour appear to have been
contained and medium-term inflation
expectations remain well anchored.
  Currently, indicators of medium-term
inflation are consistent with inflation
returning to target.
But the longer inflation remains high,
the greater the risk of higher costs
feeding into inflation expectations
and price- and wage-setting
behaviour, making inflation more
persistent.

### Page 7

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting

The balance of
risks for
economic growth
and inflation
New Zealand’s economic recovery
could be stronger or weaker than
expected and price pressures could
generate more persistent inflation.
New Zealand’s recovery could be
stronger than expected but could
also lose momentum if the global
environment deteriorates or export
strength does not flow through to
jobs, incomes and household
spending.
  Consumption growth could be
subdued if employment and house
price growth remain weak, or if
precautionary behaviour persists.
There is a risk that inflation becomes
entrenched before conventional
expectations measures respond,
despite the previous two OCR
increases.
  Alternatively, consumption growth
could be stronger and absorb spare
capacity faster than currently
anticipated.
In addition, the world economy could
tip abruptly into a downturn causing
weaker inflation over the medium
term.
  All members agreed that downside
risks to activity were significant and
that the recovery could remain
uneven.
It is also possible that geopolitical
escalation could drive fuel prices
higher while weakening global
economic activity at the same time.

Key arguments
advanced by
more hawkish
MPC members
Hayley Gourley, Karen Silk,
Prasanna Gai and Anna Breman
saw upside risks to inflation relative
to the central projection.
Hayley Gourley, Paul Conway and
Karen Silk favoured increasing the
OCR by 25 basis points at this
meeting.

### Page 8

Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
  These members observed that more
persistence in energy and
petrochemical prices could raise
near-term inflation risks, impact
price-setting behaviour, and lead to
more sticky inflation over the
medium term.
These members emphasised that
core inflation has been above levels
consistent with headline inflation at
the target mid-point for some time.
  Price-setting can be a front-loaded
process, meaning businesses could
raise domestic prices more than
import costs warrant.
This increases the risk of inflation
becoming entrenched in price-
setting behaviour as the conflict and
its consequences endure, including
through higher freight and shipping
costs.
  These factors increase the risk that
monetary policy needs to lean
against broader inflation pressures.
Acting now would guard against
sustained inflation pressures and
potentially reduce the risk of needing
to do more with interest rates later.

Key arguments
advanced by
more dovish MPC
members in each
meeting
Paul Conway and Carl Hansen saw
risks to inflation as balanced.
Anna Breman, Carl Hansen,
Rebecca Williams and Prasanna Gai
favoured holding the OCR at this
meeting as two recent OCR
increases allow the Committee to
take further time to assess their
effects and economic developments.
  These members shared concerns
that high inflation could become
embedded through price-setting
behaviour.
Actual inflation was lower than
expected, reflecting smaller indirect
effects of higher fuels prices, and
medium-term inflation expectations
and wage growth remain consistent
with returning inflation to the target
mid-point.

### Page 9

Related articles
Category 2 September 2026 meeting 28 October 2026 meeting
But weighed against this, they saw
activity risks as skewed to the
downside and noted that these could
weigh on inflation.
These members emphasised the
risk of a weak recovery, particularly
in household consumption and
investment.
They highlighted the potential effects
of weak house prices and
precautionary behaviour, weakening
growth in household spending.
They favoured pausing at this

meeting to observe and assess
demand and financial conditions.

## Document 349: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

## Document 428: The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The FOMC pause to reflect (2026-11-06).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#fomc #usd #us
The FOMC pause to reflect
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
08:14 October 29 2026 Share
No change in October, though confidence in the economy's strength and two dissents point
to a hike in December.
The FOMC held at their October meeting, albeit with two dissents, Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed
President Hammack preferring to hike by 25bps at this meeting. The statement was short and, in the
press conference, Chair Warsh again declined to provide definitive guidance on the near-term policy

### Page 2

Related articles
outlook.
The Committee remains positive on economic activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding
at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Despite elevated uncertainty, “domestic
spending has been resilient”, underpinned by “strong” productivity growth and “robust” capital
investment. Chair Warsh subsequently also emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment. Labour demand and supply were represented as balanced and resilient in

both the statement and press conference.
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains
elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual
consumer inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
Along with the two dissents, this guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help the
Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter,

the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through 2027.
Towards the end of the press conference, Chair Warsh also announced a structural change in the
FOMC schedule. While there will still be 8 meetings in 2027, a meeting in the fall will focus on
monetary policy strategy versus the current stance of monetary policy. Chair Warsh then intends for 2
of the 8 meetings in 2028 to have this strategy focus. If realised, this will see the FOMC review and

vote on the current stance of monetary policy just 6 times per calendar year from 2028.

收73億美元續創歷史新高、年增78%，2027年相關

年減5%。英特爾預估 2026年個人電腦出貨量將年
減12%、2027年受到記憶體與儲存供給影響預估將
續減10%。
受到上半年消費者預期終端售價上漲提前換機，
3Q26個人電腦與智慧手機出貨均呈現大幅下滑。根
據IDC統計，3Q26全球個人電腦出貨量達6,270萬
台，較2025年同期衰退20%。同樣受到記憶體漲價
與供應短缺衝擊的智慧手機3Q26出貨量2.6億台，
較2025年同期衰退22%。由於未來記憶體與儲存價
格小幅調漲以及供給受限下，消費終端價格仍將維
持高價位。因此，IDC預估個人電腦與智慧手機
2027年將高機率維持衰退，預估要2028年出貨量
才有望復甦。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/30，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

吸納量
重點摘要
3
《類比晶片終端需求上升 ，工業、資料中心占比較
高供應商營收更具成長性》
德州儀器(TXN)3Q26營收60.6億美元、EPS 2.62美
元雙雙優於預期，4Q26財測營收58-63億美元，中
位數較市場普遍預期高出 6%，反映資料中心市場
需求持續成長 ，使營收預測優於往年季節性回落 。
以3Q26終端市場來看 ，工業營收年增超過 40%、
季增約10%，成長來自航太國防 、能源基礎設施等
領域；車用營收年增約 15%、季增約10%，受益於
客戶庫存水準降低及電動車占比提高；資料中心營
收則年增約95%、季增20%，為成長最快的終端市
場。在營運方面，產能利用率提高以及2027年潛在
的價格調漲可能進一步擴大獲利成長空間 ，儘管資

本支出可能高於2026年的20~30億美元，但顯示終
端需求正擴大成長。
恩智浦(NXPI)3Q26營收37.9億美元、EPS 4.20美元
優於預期 ，但4Q26財測營收中位數 38.5億美元略
低於市場預期 。以3Q26終端市場來看 ，車用營收
20.7億美元、年增13%，成長主要來自軟體定義汽
車(SDV)產品貢獻；工業與物聯網營收 8.02億美元 、
年增39%，主要由資料中心 、工廠自動化和醫療保
健等產業帶動；通訊基礎設施營收 5.03億美元、年
增54%，得益於資料中心業務成長 。恩智浦財報結
果顯示各終端市場需求持續上升 ，惟成長速度較慢
的車用營收占比超過五成 ，未來成長速度可能不及
工業和資料中心營收占比更高的同業。

4
《券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期持續增溫》
券商 CIO 調查指出 2027 年的企業 IT 採購預算仍維
持成長。CIO 現在預計 2026 年的 IT 採購預算將年
增 4.0%，自 2Q26 走高 20bps，連續 3 次增溫。
對於 2027 年 IT 採購預算的首次估計，CIO 預期年
增 4.0%，雖僅與更新後的 2026 年預期相符，但實
則是近 4 年來的開年新高。在這之中，軟體預算成
長優於領先硬體、網通與服務，預期年增 4.4%，且

自 2026 年加速 10bps。2027 年軟體支出的預期加
速可能進一步說明短期內 AI 並未帶來全面性衝擊。
資料來源：摩根史丹利，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/20
人工智慧與機器學習 (AI/ML) 的支出優先性仍穩居
CIO 調查的第一名，佔比雖較前幾期調研回落，但
這應該是因為 CIO 對於開始增加其他項目的優先性。
AI 相關專案的預算仍最不可能被刪除，且淨占比
(即最不可能被刪除 – 最可能被刪除的比例)，3Q26
較 2Q26 大幅走揚 13ppts 至 17%。網路安全的支
出優先性則連 4 季走揚，且最不可能被刪除的淨占
比也走揚 1ppt 至 16%。持續反映出 AI 帶動的攻擊
面擴大、攻擊手法更加多元，與攻擊成本大幅走低，
導致企業在 AI 顯著產生正面效益前，優先選擇防範
未然的心態。而隨著 AI 的能力仍在快速發展，如近
期 Anthropic 研究即指出 AI 已可自我迭代，且小
型模型未來可能具備在物聯網裝置上自我複製的能

力，本行預期網安的需求可望仍持續強健。數位轉
型雖仍處第 3，但在本次券商調查中大幅地低於過
硬體 軟體 通訊設備 IT服務

72 收藏 分享到 :
特朗普被记者问及万斯的表态时说： “ 我认为这在很⼤程度上是真的，因为它（伊朗战事）
在短期内推⾼了汽油价格。 ” 他还说，从⼀开始就知道万斯对伊朗战事的看法。美国媒体解
读，万斯在此节点上发⽂显然是与美国总统特朗普做切割，开始为参加 2028 年总统竞选布
局。
摘要：
美国副总统万斯 4 ⽇在社交媒体上发表⻓⽂，剖析共和党在中期选举中失利的原因，直⾔伊朗战
事是根源所在，并提出改变美国与以⾊列的关系。美国媒体解读，万斯在此节点上发⽂显然是
与美国总统特朗普做切割，开始为参加 2028 年总统竞选布局。
万斯在⽂章中写道，数据专家对他说， “ 很多 2024 年投票给特朗普和万斯的选⺠很愤怒，正打算
投给另⼀边 ” 。他认为， “ 接下来会有很多内⽃和争论，争论发⽣了什么、为什么会这样 …… 但我

们输掉的真正原因，⼏乎所有⼈都⼼知肚明。 ”
万斯坦⾔，他在中期选举前 “ 已经感觉不妙 ” ，并援引⺠调专家的观点提出让选⺠回归共和党的四
项任务，即结束伊朗战事、聚焦⺠众⽣活成本和通胀问题、坚持 “ 美国优先 ” 、改变美以关系。他
还直接点明， “ 我们失利的原因就是这场战争 …… 每个⼈都知道我们为什么输了 ” 。
当天晚些时候，特朗普被记者问及万斯的表态时说： “ 我认为这在很⼤程度上是真的，因为它
（伊朗战事）在短期内推⾼了汽油价格。 ” 他还说，从⼀开始就知道万斯对伊朗战事的看法。
美国有线电视新闻⽹⽂章分析认为，万斯的帖⽂ “ ⾮同寻常 ” ，包含对特朗普 “ 不加掩饰的批
评 ” ，他实际上是在与特朗普切割，以谋求能够在 2028 年竞选总统。万斯此举将 “ 成为特朗普时
代的⼀个转折点 ” 。
美国福克斯新闻频道报道说，⽩宫 4 ⽇上午召集政府官员开会，要求在中期选举后统⼀信息发布
⼝径，但 “ 万斯掌控了⾃⼰的信息发布，在外交政策和美以关系上与特朗普分道扬镳 ” 。报道援引

⼀名特朗普政府官员的话说，万斯提出的四项任务是他对美国 “ 新右翼 ” 发出的集结号，但他 “ 并
⾮像前副总统彭斯那样与特朗普决裂 ” 。 “ 新右翼 ” 的诉求被定义为不卷⼊海外冲突、让美国成为
⽣活可负担的地⽅以及关闭边境。

景気は弱い動き
中国景気は弱い動きが続いているが、改善の兆しもみられる。2026年7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5%
だった。前期（前年比＋4.3%）から伸びが拡大し、1～9月累計で同＋4.6%と政府目標の「4.5～5%」の範囲内で推移した。
ただし、10月の製造業PMIは49.3と前月から低下し、好不況の境目である50を2カ月ぶりに下回った。構成指数をみると、
生産と新規受注の低下が全体を押し下げた。非製造業PMIも49.7と低下し、50を下回った。

固定資産投資は減少、生産は底堅く推移
9月の固定資産投資（年初来累計値）は前年比－7.2%と大幅な減少が続いた。もっとも、製造業の減少幅が縮小し、全体
の減少幅は前月と同じだった。また、季節調整済み前月比は7カ月ぶりにプラスとなるなど、下げ止まりの兆しがみられる。
9月の工業生産は前年比＋5.5%と増加し、底堅く推移している。コンピュータ・通信、その他電子設備製造業（前年比
＋16.4%）や生産用機械製造業（同+12.1%）を中心に、ハイテク製造業（同＋15.3%）が全体を押し上げた。

輸出、輸入ともに大幅増加が続く
9月の輸出は前年比＋36.3%、輸入は同＋31.0%と、ともに大幅な増加が続いた。輸出はASEAN向け（前年比＋52.3%）
や香港向け（同＋51.0%）、米国向け（同＋39.6%）をはじめ、多くの国・地域向けで高い伸びを記録した。品目別では、石
油製品（同＋113.8%）や電子部品（同＋92.4%） 、PC類（同＋83.4%）の増加寄与が大きかった。輸入は、ASEANなどア
ジア（同＋43.5%）からの増加が目立った。貿易黒字は1,335億ドルと前年同月（904億ドル）から大きく拡大した。

為替は元高、株価は下落
人民元の対ドルレート（10月末）は1ドル＝6.66元と、月末値としては2022年5月以来の元高水準となった。大幅な貿易黒
字に加え、米長期金利の低下を受けて、ドル安・元高が進んだ。9月末の外貨準備高は3兆4,003億ドルと減少した。
10月の上海総合株価指数は、中東情勢の先行き不透明感の高まりに加え、世界的な半導体株安などを背景に、月間を
通じて下落した。業種別指数は、2025年以降の株価上昇を牽引してきた情報や通信の下落が大きかった。

2026年7～9月期のGDP 外需の強さを背景に製造業が成長率を押し上げ
2026年7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は、前期から伸びが拡大した（1頁参照）。需要項目別にみると、総資本形成の増加
寄与が大きく縮小し、内需寄与度は低下したものの、輸出の大幅増加を背景に外需寄与度が拡大した（左図）。
業種別にみると、旺盛な外需に支えられ、製造業の伸びが高まったほか、前年同期の伸びが小さかった小売・卸売や宿
泊・飲食も増加幅の拡大が目立った（右図）。一方、不動産不況が長引く中、建設業は6四半期連続で、不動産業は5四半
期連続で前年割れとなった。

国慶節の人出は増加も、財布のひもは依然固い
中国の建国記念日である国慶節（10/1）に伴う大型連休（10/1～7）の1日当たり国内旅行者数は、前年比＋6.3%と増加
した（左図）。休日日数は前年より1日短かったものの、今年は中秋節の連休が9月末にあり、間で休暇を取れば最大13連
休となる日並びだった。このため、海外旅行需要も高まり、連休中の1日当たり出入国者数は、前年比＋8.1%と増加した。
もっとも、連休中の1人1日当たり旅行消費額は2024年以降伸び悩んでおり、今年は前年比－1.9%と2年連続で減少した
（右図）。旅行需要は底堅いものの、消費者の節約志向は依然として根強い。

国慶節連休での1日当たり国内旅行者数 国慶節連休での1人1日当たり旅行消費額

## Document 616: 凱基投顧_3533 嘉澤_向子慧_20261103

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_3533 嘉澤_向子慧_20261103.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 03, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  連接器 ‧ 台灣

嘉澤 
(3533.TW /3533 TT)

儘管近期利潤率逆風，但來年仍有數項動能  
重要訊息 
嘉澤將於 11/12 日公布 3Q26 財報，11 /13 日法說。預期毛利率於 3Q26 落
底，2027 年伺服器展望強勁且利潤率回升，2H27- 202 年另有多項新動能。 
評論及分析 
2027-28 年三大催化劑將推動獲利成長。我們預期三大催化劑將推動 2027-28
年強勁的獲利成長展望：(1) 受代理式 AI 應用帶動 CPU 需求強勁，以及 AMD 
(美) Venice與 Intel (美) Oak Stream等新 CPU 將於 2H26至 1H27推出，我們預
期 Venice 與 Oak Stream CPU 明年出貨量均將年增逾 30%。再加上平均針腳
數將增加逾 20%至 7,000根針以上，並搭配 PCIe Gen 6 及 DDR6 Socket 連接
器營收擴張，我們預期嘉澤伺服器相關營收 2027-28 年仍將維持逾 50%的年
成長率。(2) 快接頭 (QD)、SOCAMM及 Cable 等新產品營收將於 2027年持續
成長，貢獻占比將由 2H26 的 5-6% (逾 10 億元) 提升至明年的 5-10% (逾 50
億元)，受惠於 AI 伺服器需求。(3) 多項開發與認證中的新產品可能於 2H27 至
2028年進入量產，包括 NPO Socket、GPU Socket及超高密度互連 (UDI)。 
2H26 利潤率疲弱態勢延續，3Q26 可望落底。受 CPU 相關零組件（記憶體、
PCB、載板）供應吃緊拖累 2H26 伺服器相關營收，嘉澤 3Q26 營收僅季增
4%，我們預估 4Q26約季增 4%。AMD Venice CPU 將於 4Q26開始放量，但
出貨至 2Q27才會顯著。我們預期 AMD與 Intel明年 CPU出貨展望強勁，成長
逾 30%，將帶動嘉澤營收於 2027年重回強勁年增。預期嘉澤 2026-27年伺服
器相關營收將維持 50-60%的高年增率，對比 1Q-3Q26 年增 63% (占總營收
53%)。毛利率方面，由於銅、金等原材料成本高於預期，加上 PC 產品產能利
用率偏低，預估 3Q26 毛利率將季減至 44.8%，低於先前預估及市場共識。由
於 4Q26 新 CPU相關營收貢獻有限，毛利率季增回升可能要到 1Q27後才會顯
著。我們下修 3Q26及 2026年 EPS預估，分別至 17.37元及 73.84元。 
2027 年 EPS 將強勁成長。受惠於新伺服器 CPU Socket 營收貢獻增加，以及
SOCAMM、QD 與 Cable 營收成長，我們預估 2027 年伺服器相關營收占比將
由 2026年的 54%提升至 64%，NPO Socket 及 Nvidia (美) GPU Socket 帶來上
行空間。Microsoft (美) ASIC CPU Cobalt 亦採用 Socket 設計，營收貢獻將於
2H27 至 2028 年增加。因此，我們預期毛利率將於明年回升至 49%、2028年
回升至 50%，推升 2027-28年 EPS至 115.5元及 168.09元。 
投資建議 
我們將目標價下調至 2,310 元，相當 2027 年每股盈餘的 20 倍。預期伺服
器營收強勁且 AI 新產品營收成長，維持「增加持股」評等。  
投資風險 
原料價格上漲、新 CPU 平台導入落後及新台幣升值。

焦點內容   
1. 3Q26 毛利率將小幅季減，低於共識預
期，但預計將為谷底。 
2. 2027 年伺服器需求仍將維持強勁，但
零組件供應將是主要限制因素。新 CPU
平台推出將帶動插槽營收擴張，推升伺
服器業務於 2026- 27 年維持 50-60%的
高速成長。 
3. 1-3Q26 伺服器相關營收年增 63%，約
占整體營收 53%。我們預期伺服器業
務持續成長將有助於推動公司於 2027-
28 年毛利率回升。
